2026 年 10 月 9 日 全9 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 8 月消費統計 
耐久財とサービスが弱く、総じて見れば前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年8 月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比＋0.1％と2 カ月
連続で増加した。 一方、複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（ CTI
ミクロ）で見た実質消費は同▲2.3％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。また、供給側統計の 1
つである商業動態統計では、CPI の財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。
総じて見れば、8 月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は 底堅く推移するとみている 。名目賃金は春闘での高水準の賃上げ
や最低賃金の引き上げもあって上昇が続く見込みである。 一方、政府の物価高対策 の
効果の剝落などが物価を押し上げる要因となろう。加えて、原油の輸入価格は代替調
達もあって高止まりしており、幅広い分野の価格への波及を通じて個人消費を下押し
する可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

＜2026 年8 月の消費総括＞耐久財やサービスを中心に減少 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比＋0.1％と2カ月
連続で小幅に増加した一方、複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTI ミク
ロ）で見た実質消費は同▲2.3％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した（図表 1）。また、供給側統計の 1 つ
である商業動態統計では、CPI の財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。総じて見
れば、2026年8月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTI ミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞「交通・通信」などの7 費目が減少 
2026年 8 月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10 大費目1のうち、 「交通・
通信」 （前月比▲5.3％） 、「その他」 （同▲4.1％）、 「教養娯楽」 （同▲2.9％） 、 「教育」 （同▲
4.7％） 、「家具・家事用品」 （同▲4.4％） 、「被服及び履物」 （同▲3.7％） 、「保健医療」 （同▲
0.5％）の7費目が減少した。 
他方、「光熱・水道」 （前月比＋4.4％） 、「住居」 （同＋3.3％） 、「食料」 （同＋0.5％）の3費目
が増加した（図表2）。 
図表 2：実質世帯消費動向指数（CTI ミクロ）の前月比

（注）二人以上の世帯。総務省による季節調整値。シェアは 2025 年の数値。

算出している。

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。 
CTI ミクロにおける「交通・通信」は 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。自動車等関係費などへの支出
が減少した。「その他」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。諸雑費やこづかいなどへの支出が減少した。
「教養娯楽」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。教養娯楽サービスを中心に、教養娯楽用品などへの支出
が減少した。 「教育」は2 カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「家具・家
事用品」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。家庭用耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は
2 カ月ぶりに減少した。洋服を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は 2 カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療サービスを中心に支出が減少した。 
他方、「光熱・水道」は 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。電気代やガス代を中心に支出が拡大した。
「住居」は 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。家賃地代を中心に支出が拡大した。「食料」は 2 カ月連続で
増加した。酒類や野菜・海藻を中心に支出が拡大した。 
家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比＋0.1％と2カ月連続で小幅に増加
した。ただし、振れの大きい項目を除いた、住居等を除くベースでは同▲1.7％と2カ月ぶりに
減少した（図表 3 上段左）。 
基礎的支出は前月比＋0.9％と、4 カ月ぶりに増加した。また、選択的支出は同＋1.3％と、2
カ月連続で増加した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表3 中段左）。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3 中段右）、耐久財
（前月比＋8.2％）が4カ月ぶりに増加し、全体を押し上げた。非耐久財（同＋0.4％）も3カ月
ぶりに増加した。一方、サービス（同▲0.3％）と半耐久財（同▲0.1％）は減少した。

2 ただし、授業料等は児童や学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さく
振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに2 カ月ぶりに減少 
2026 年 8 月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲1.2％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少
した（図表 4、5） 。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、全ての業種が前月から減少した。「機械器具小売業」 （前
月比▲6.9％）は 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。「自動車小売業」 （同▲2.5％）は 4 カ月連続で減少し
た。8 月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同▲7.6％と減少しており3、この結
果とも整合的だ。「燃料小売業」 （同▲1.8％）は2カ月ぶりに減少した。「飲食料品小売業」 （同
▲0.4％）は2カ月ぶりに減少した。コンビニの加工食品（同▲1.5％）が減少した。「織物・衣
服・身の回り品小売業」 （同▲1.8％）、「その他小売業」 （同▲0.2％）、「各種商品小売業」 （同
▲0.4％）はいずれも2カ月ぶりに減少した。

の小売業」 。

＜2026 年9 月の消費＞8 月から減少した可能性 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB 消費 NOW をもとに判断すると、2026 年 9 月の消費は 8 月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、9 月前半の実績をもとに試算したスーパーの JCB 消費額（大
和総研による季節調整値）と家電の JCB 消費額（同）は減少した。一方、アパレルの売上高は
堅調に推移し、新車販売台数（同）も増加した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB 消費額（同）
と外食の JCB 消費額（同）は天候不順もあっていずれも前月から減少した。一方、新幹線輸送
量の前年比伸び率は前月から拡大した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の伸びが高水準で推移する一方、
政府の物価高対策による物価の押し下げ効果の剝落などで、物価上昇率が高まり、実質賃金が
伸び悩む可能性がある。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。春闘の結果5は前年をやや下回るものの、高水準
となった。また、 2026 年度の最低賃金の 引き上げ率は全国加重平均で＋ 5.0％（前年度は＋
6.3％）と高水準を維持した6。 
消費者物価について、7 月使用分から 9 月使用分まで実施される電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が終了した。加えて、代替調達もあって原油の輸入価格は高止まりして
おり7、原材料費や輸送費の上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、消費を下押しする可能性
には注意が必要だ。帝国データバンクによると、値上げされた飲食料品のうち中東情勢の影響
を受けた品目の割合は 9 月末時点で約 3 割だった。今後も値上げが継続する見込みだが、値上
げの品目数は10月で一旦ピークアウトする可能性もある8。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある9。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、9 月の消費者態度指数（二人以上
の世帯、季節調整値 ）は 35.4（前月差▲0.1pt）と小幅に低下し、依然として低水準にあり
（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照） 、節約志向の高まりに注意が必要だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/10/6 号）」（大和総研レポート、2026 年10月 6 日）を参照。 
5 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3 年連続の 5％超え！～2026 春季生活闘争 第7 回（最終）回答集計結果
について～」（2026 年7 月3日）によると、定昇相当込みの賃上げ率の平均は 5.01％（前年同時期は
5.25％）となった。 
6 厚生労働省「地域別最低賃金の全国一覧」（2026 年10 月9 日閲覧） 
7 世界的な需給の引き締まりも受け、ホルムズ海峡を経由しないルートでの中東や他地域からの原油の代替調
達の価格が割高となり、調達コストが押し上げられている。詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田
凱「日本経済見通し：2026 年8 月」（大和総研レポート、2026 年8 月 24 日）を参照。 
8 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195 社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026 年 10 月 飲食料品値上げ、10 月は3153
品目 値上げラッシュはピーク越え」（2026 年9 月30 日） 
9 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026 年8 月」（大和総研レポート、

Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
UMC (2303.TW) From To
Price Target NT$138.00 NT$188.00
TSMC (2330.TW) From To
Price Target NT$2,988.00 NT$3,088.00
We think the capex budget might gradually shift to AI compute
and AI networking in 2028, as memory usage needs to be
optimized. We see positives for TSMC and UMC.
TSMC 3Q26 earnings preview: We expect the company to raise full-year revenue
guidance to the low-to-mid-40% range (vs.  above 40%) Y/Y, implying 4Q26 revenue

will rise 10%-15% Q/Q amid very strong 3nm demand. 3Q26 gross margin should
reach the high end of guidance of 67.5% However, should Intel focus on internal
capacity expansion, we think TSMC would maintain 2nm capacity expansion at the
same pace and its capex at US$54bn for 2026. For 2027, we see TSMC’s capex
reaching US$75bn to US$80bn.
In terms of 2027 wafer price hikes, we see leading edge (7nm and below) up 5%-10%
and mature nodes up 0%-3%; hence, gross margin could approach 70% in 2027. As a

result, we raise  our TSMC price target  by 3% to NT$3,088.
Key topics for upcoming TSMC 3Q26 earnings call on October 15:
1. Would TSMC consider working with TereFab  (Taiwan News, Oct 3 2026) and
building new fab sites in Texas (Bloomberg, Oct 1 2026)? Our view is that expansion
in Texas could be necessary, considering customer demand and limited space at the
Arizona fab in five years.
2. If Intel were to increase capex, would that reduce outsourcing at TSMC? Our

recent industry checks suggest some trimming of Intel’s 3nm and 2nm; how might
TSMC reallocate its resources?
3. How would TSMC address large reticle size chip demand? Our checks suggest
MediaTek handled TPU v10 IceFish reticle size initially at 14x; could TSMC’s CoWoS
or CoPoS be able to support such packaging?
We raise  our UMC price target by 36% to NT$188 on AI networking (SiPh)
business upside: Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone

and PC demand weakness. We have seen some order cuts in PC semis recently, as
some consumer analog IC customers (e.g., Silergy) have not been able to pass
through UMC’s wafer price hike. Therefore, some investors do not expect any UMC’s
price hike in 2027. However, two growth drivers could help UMC raise wafer price
for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, as we noted in our earlier  Foundry
Floorplan, and 2) growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 5, 2026 08:00 PM GMT

TSMC's 2026–28 foundry floorplan broadly aligns with our
bottom-up demand analysis
Our industry checks suggest ASML allocates capacity based largely on historical demand
and long-term partnerships, indicating that TSMC remains the largest customer for EUV
shipments. If so, TSMC should retain strong pricing power and could increase leading-edge
wafer prices by 5%–10% in 2027, reflecting both the value it delivers to customers and the
potential for margin expansion.

TSMC 2nm and 3nm capacity growth remains robust; 5nm
set to decline
Consistent with our demand forecast, TSMC indicated very strong capacity requirements
for both 2nm and 3nm starting in 2026, implying a 70% capacity CAGR for N2 from 2026

to 2028. TSMC also suggested that 5nm capacity will begin to decline from 2027, in line
with our forecast that 5nm demand will drop as Blackwell transitions to Rubin on 3nm.
Separately, advanced packaging capacity expansion should remain in focus for TSMC in
2027. This includes, but is not limited to, CoWoS and SoIC, for which we now expect
capacity to reach 200kwpm and 40kwpm, respectively, by 2027.

How might TSMC address Intel's competition in both wafer
foundry and advanced packaging?
Intel EMIB-T remains competitive in 2029; how TSMC will
address?
With demand in AI continuing to boom, we expect TSMC and its OSAT partners to
expedite capacity expansion plans for CoWoS. We see TSMC's CoWoS capacity building
further to 260kwpm in 2028, from 130/200kwpm in 2026/2027, with 2028's expansion
mainly focused in Arizona's AP9/10 and potentially in AP7. TSMC is now conducting trial

production for CoPoS in Taoyuan. If the progress goes well, we would expect TSMC to
start building its CoPoS capacity, with location also likely to be in AP9/10 or AP7P3.
We expect the non-TSMC camp to expand CoWoS capacity to 110kwpm by 2028 year-end,
from 80kwpm by end-2027. For ASE/SPIL, we expect FoCoS+CoWoS capacity to rise from
30kwpm at 2027-end to 50-60kwpm at 2028-end, with expansion focused on CoWoS-L
and CoWoS-R. For Amkor, we expect CoWoS capacity to increase from 30kwpm at 2027-

end to 55-60kwpm at 2028-end, also focused on CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R.

What about  Intel EMIB capacity build?
For Intel, we see current average EMIB-M capacity of 110kwpm falling slightly to 95kwpm
in 2027 as it converts some toward EMIB-T. We see the major customers of EMIB-M being
Trainium3, upcoming Trainium 4 and its in-house server CPU. Average EMIB-T capacity is

around 5kwpm in 2026, which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-
45kwpm in 2028. All of the capacity mentioned above is converted for  12-inch wafer
equivalent, which we believe would be much more easier to communicate with compared
to TSMC CoWoS capacity.
Although some small projects are ongoing, we still see MediaTek/Google Humufish
consuming most of the capacity during 2027-28. We maintain our view that Intel EMIB-T

Indeed, we have seen some TSMC customers surveying (testing vehicles) Intel EMIB-T due
to the design for larger chip size, lower cost, and intention to seek a second source
because TSMC's CoWoS capacity is still relatively tight. However, we believe TSMC could
still try to expand its packaging size by leveraging different technologies to meet
customers' needs. We think it remains concerned about whether it is going to open its
front-end computing die production/know-how to its competitor, Intel, for back-end

packaging.
Our prediction of the trend after 2028 –  TSMC might:
1. Pull in CoPoS (panel-level process) with both 300mm and 500mm square panel
size
2. Introduce 3.5D packaging with SoIC for vertical chip stacking (Meta MTIA is
surveying)
3. Adopt glass core substrate to solve warpage for >9.7x reticle-size chips

Pros and cons of TSMC's CoWoS, Intel's EMIB, TSMC's
CoPoS, and SoIC
TSMC’s CoWoS, Intel’s EMIB, TSMC’s CoPoS, and TSMC’s SoIC represent different points
along the advanced packaging spectrum, each optimizing for a different balance of
bandwidth, cost, scalability and integration complexity. We think future design of the
chips is likely to combine these approaches—for example, using CoWoS for lateral

integration and SoIC for vertical stacking—to achieve optimal system-level performance.
Exhibit 12: Comparison between TSMC's CoWoS, CoPoS, SoIC, and Intel's EMIB-T
CoWoS CoPoS SoIC EMIB
2.5D 2.5D 3D 2.5D
Wafer level Panel level Chip/die stacking Substrate level

Efficiency for large chip (use of the subrate area) Low High NA High

Cost at 9.7x retical size (including yield issue) High Theoretical low Highest Theoret ical low
Technology maturity (opposite of execution risk) High Low Medium Low
Thermal challenge Medium Theoretical low Highest Theoretical low

In summary,

### Page 9

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 9
• CoWoS excels in high-bandwidth 2.5D integration but is costly and capacity-limited
• EMIB offers a more cost-effective and modular alternative with some performance
trade-offs
• CoPoS targets large-scale, cost-efficient integration but is still maturing
• SoIC delivers the highest performance through 3D stacking at the expense of
complexity and thermal constraints.

10
UMC is well positioned for Silicon Photonic IC (PIC) foundry
In our previous Foundry Floorplan, we presented a constructive view on UMC given its AI-
related revenue. Management indicated AI revenue of US$300mn this year (3-5% of
revenue), potentially more than tripling to US$1bn in three years (2029).
UMC has a  capacity expansion plan to capture AI demand, including a clean room for
advanced packaging for AI chips, such as wafer-on-wafer and memory-on-memory stacking.

Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone and PC demand
weakness, and we have seen some order cuts of PC semis recently. Some think a UMC
price hike in 2027 wouldn’t go through, but we see two growth drivers helping it
successfully  hike wafer price for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, and 2)
growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its 400Gb per lane solution
(PIC + TFLN) solution.
How UMC is differentiated in PIC foundry

According to UMC, InP and TFLN are the two most promising solutions, as they are much
more mature than the other contenders. InP is facing severe shortage, adding to TFLN’s
momentum.
UMC Group’s TFLN program started in 2021 with 6” in mass production and 8” in
sampling. TFLN's   strengths include low loss, high bandwidth (> 110 GHz), good thermal
stability, and strong linearity.
Integrating TFLN with PIC is a challenge as well as an opportunity. Advanced packaging

schemes and micro transfer printing are both seen as promising candidates. UMC is
developing the integration technology, leveraging its expertise in PIC, TFLN, and advanced
packaging.
Adoption of higher-performance modulators (TFLN/InP) and improvement in photo diodes
are key factors affecting deployment of plain-vanilla (non-WDM, IM/DD) DCIs with 400
Gbps/channel performance and beyond, which is very close to TSMC's mainstream PIC
technology Micro Ring.

About Foundry Floorplan
In 2011,  we first published this product (Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan) to
provide our analysis of foundry industry dynamics. We have resumed this practice as
global foundry competition remains active. Foundry supply is also a critical resource for
future AI chips, whether in the global market or China's local market.
Below are prior reports from this series:
• The Foundry Floorplan: All boats riding on the same tide – mature node up-cycle

begins (19 May 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs. EMIB competition (14
Apr 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Raise TSMC's 2027e and 2028e Capex (9 Mar 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Update on TSMC's 2027 capex; interposer outsourcing to
Vanguard? (13 Jan 2026)
• Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan, April 2011

TSMC: Estimate and price target revisions
Our earnings forecasts increase 4% for 2026,  5% for  2027 and 3% for 2028: We raise
our 2026 revenue forecast and modestly increase our margin assumptions to reflect
stronger-than-expected 3Q26 performance, driven by robust AI demand, including
sustained Blackwell shipments. We raise our 2027–28 revenue assumptions by 3%–5%,
reflecting the increase in strong leading-edge demand as well as the wafer price hike.

Our PT increases to NT$3,088 from   NT$2,988, driven primarily by our estimate

revisions. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027 EPS forecast. TSMC
currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of 16.5x since 2018. We
find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an

intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase to NT$3,720 (from NT$3,600) and NT
$1,700 (from NT$1,645), respectively.

20x 2027e EPS

foundry services with some gross margin
expansion: 1) Global semi revenue CAGR is

decrease meaningfully in 3nm and 2nm,
while capex per k is more demanding than
for 7nm and 5nm.

and/or Samsung successfully develop

foundry business execution proves

competition. 5) Demand for 2nm is less than
expected given high transistor cost for
foundry customers.

MS Rating
▪ We are OW as we see structural changes
in the mature node industry's
supply/demand dynamics.
▪ Its foundry quality is comparable to
TSMC's in mature nodes. Along with

specialty technologies, this could help offset
part of the margin downside.
▪ Non-AI market recovery remains uncertain
in 2027, yet UMC should still beneﬁt from
strong AI-related demand spillover from its
foundry peers.
▪ 12nm FinFET development is strategic. It
should be one of UMC's long-term
strengths, notably when Chinese foundries
cannot effectively expand FinFET capacity.
▪ The stock now trades at 4.1x 2027e BVPS.
That is well above its historical average

since 2009, but our price target implies 5.1x.

NT$270.00
7.5x 2027e BVPS
Stabilized semi demand; better-than-
expected 8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow >15% Y/Y

in 2026, and ex-TSMC foundry industry
grows >10% Y/Y; 2) UMC gains share in both
8- and 12-inch wafers thanks to technology
differentiation; 3) UMC receives more
outsourcing from customers, which helps ﬁll
22/28nm capacity and lifts blended wafer
ASP; 4) traction increases for UMC's
12nm/14nm FinFET technologies.
NT$188.00
5.2x 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi growth; improving demand for
8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch: 1) Global
semi sales grow ~15% in 2026 and ex-TSMC

foundry industry grows 3-5% Y/Y; 2) 8-inch
utilization rate is above 75% with steady 12-
inch demand amid semi inventory digestion;
3) UMC hikes wafer prices slightly in
response to strong AI-related demand.
NT$95.00
2.6x 2027e BVPS
Signiﬁcant semi industry decline; 8-inch and
trailing-edge 12-inch face weakening
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow only <5%
in 2026; 2) display semi and power semi
demand deteriorates further; 3) signiﬁcant
wafer pricing erosion given low fab

utilization and mature node peers'
competition; 4) depreciation ramp-up of
more than 30% Y/Y in 2026 without
sufﬁcient wafer order inﬂow to sustain
UMC's gross margin.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

M
Update
Hiwin Technologies Corp.  | Asia Pacific
September Sales Flat MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Hiwin's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$2,720mn (flat MoM/+30% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$8,110mn (+8% QoQ/+35% YoY), largely in line
with our estimate at NT$7,996mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY) and the  consensus
of NT$7,964mn (+6% QoQ/+33% YoY).
Our view:
• On top of the sequential growth on the top line, we believe that Hiwin

should also further expand its gross margin on a QoQ basis in 3Q26, owing
to the higher utilization and the tailwinds of price hikes.
• We think the momentum should remain favorable in the near term.
• We stay constructive on the stock, with current valuation at 29x 2027e P/E
vs. a peak cycle level of 35-40x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple
of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.

n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

按美元计，9月我国进口同比增长31%，前值增28.2%；出口同比增长36.3%，前值增25%。9月份贸易顺差
1335.32亿美元，前值1190.85亿美元。9月进出口同比增速较上月有所抬升，继续保持较快增长。出口方面，
产品结构持续优化。从主要出口市场看，9月我国前三大出口市场分别是东南亚联盟、美国和欧盟，其中，
对东盟出口同比增长52.26%，对欧盟出口增长39.56%，对美国出口增长25.25%，对美国、欧盟出口大幅改

善，对东盟及“一带一路”沿线国家出口保持高速增长。从产品结构看，出口产品持续向智向绿方向转型，
机电产品特别是集成电路同比增长显著。进口方面，随着高质量共建“一带一路”扎实推进，我国与全球
贸易伙伴的经贸合作持续深化，优质产品持续进入我国市场。从进口产品结构看，金属矿砂等大宗商品进
口同比增速持续提升；自动数据处理设备及其零部件等机电产品同比增长显著，或能说明高新产业的高出
口需求支撑了进口的景气。展望未来，近期积极因素有所积累，但美元强势带来的去杠杆效应能否缓解，
仍取决于地缘局势能否明朗。具体来看，出口方面重点关注可能增加的贸易壁垒。进口方面，经济政策的
回归或仍需和外部环境相协调，关注后续国内相应政策的落地。

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.
Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

⻩仁勋讲话后，早盘曾跌 9.5%CoreWeave 股价转涨。该司五年期 CDS 周四盘中升破 1190
基点，反映债务违约保护成本的指标创历史新⾼，部分公司债收益率升⾄ 15% ⾄ 16% 以
上。甲⻣⽂、亚⻢逊、⾕歌⺟公司、 Meta 的五年期 CDS 利差均处历史⾼位。德银分析师认
为，随着 AI 资本开⽀周期越来越多地由债务、⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流⽀撑，投资者必须更重视信
⽤市场的价格信号，整体⽽⾔， AI 相关融资成本尚不算⾼，但已有些令⼈担忧的迹象。
摘要：
CoreWeave 违约⻛险飙升⾄历史新⾼、信⽤市场隐含的未来五年违约概率超过六成之际，英伟
达 CEO ⻩仁勋出⾯ “ 灭⽕ ” ，称 CoreWeave 财务实⼒持续改善、融资成本正在下降，英伟达没有
必要为其兜底。
美东时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇周四，反映公司债务违约保护成本的指标 CoreWeave 五年期信⽤违约互换
（ CDS ）利差⼀度上升 74.5 个基点，达到 1194.5 个基点，创盘中历史新⾼。与此同时，该公司

部分债券收益率升⾄ 15% ⾄ 16% 以上，反映投资者对其偿债能⼒的担忧急剧升温。
⻩仁勋同⽇在瑞典出席活动期间接受媒体采访时表⽰： “CoreWeave 的实⼒与⽇俱增。 ” 他称，⾃
⼰对该公司持乐观态度，并指出，该司的 “ 资本成本实际上正在下降 ” 。对于市场关注的英伟达是
否需要为 CoreWeave 提供财务兜底，⻩仁勋明确表⽰，他并不认为有此必要。
周四， CoreWeave （ CRWV ）股价⼤幅低开 6.6% ，早盘跌幅⼀度扩⼤⾄ 9.5% ，⻩仁勋讲话后，
加速收窄跌幅，午盘转涨后持续上⾏，尾盘⽇内涨幅曾略超 6% ，较早盘刷新的⽇低反弹约
17% 。到收盘，股价涨逾 5.9% ，扭转三⽇连跌，本周内的累计跌幅收窄到 15% 左右。
更值得警惕的是，信⽤市场的紧张情绪在蔓延，显⽰投资者正重新评估 AI 基础设施投资背后的债
务⻛险。媒体指出，甲⻣⽂五年期 CDS 利差周四已接近 300 个基点，本周四⽇升约 31 个基点，和
亚⻢逊、⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 、 Meta 的相关利差均处于历史⾼位；软银五年期 CDS 利差⼀⽇扩

⼤ 24 个基点；英伟达、意法半导体和 SpaceX 的 CDS 利差也明显⾛⾼。

### Page 2

五年期 CDS 突破 1190 基点， CoreWeave 债券遭抛售
CoreWeave 正遭遇股债双杀。
ICE Data Services 数据显⽰，周四 CoreWeave 五年期 CDS 利差盘中升⾄ 1194.5 个基点，创有记
录以来新⾼，相关定价对应的未来五年累计违约概率超过 60% 。 LSEG 及 S&P Global Market
Intelligence 数据显⽰， CoreWeave 的 CDS 利差周四盘中突破 1100 个基点，对应的五年期违约⻛
险约为 64% ，⾼于⼀年前约 30% 的⽔平。
CDS 是⽤于对冲债务违约⻛险的信⽤衍⽣品，其利差通常随市场对发⾏⼈偿债能⼒的担忧加剧
⽽上升。不过， CDS 隐含的违约概率取决于定价模型及相关假设，并不意味着公司未来五年实
际违约概率已被精确测定。
债券市场的表现同样严峻。彭博援引⾏情数据称，周四盘中， CoreWeave 于 2031 年到期、票⾯
利率为 9% 的债券价格下跌约 3.3 美分，跌⾄每美元⾯值 79.9 美分，收益率升⾄ 15.6% ；另⼀只票

⾯利率为 9.75% 、同样于 2031 年到期的债券下跌约 2.6 美分，跌⾄ 78.65 美分，收益率升⾄
16.2% 。
路透报道称， CoreWeave 于 2032 年到期的债券在 Tradeweb 平台上的交易价格约为每美元⾯值
78 美分，收益率超过 16% 。不同债券的到期⽇、票⾯利率和交易价格有所不同，但都显⽰出投
资者正在要求更⾼的⻛险补偿。
债券价格⼤幅低于⾯值、收益率飙升，意味着 CoreWeave 未来通过债券市场融资或再融资可能
⾯临更⾼成本。
CoreWeave 联合创始⼈ Brannin McBee ⽇前接受《⾦融时报》采访时表⽰，公司希望获得投资
级信⽤评级，从⽽打开更⼴泛的融资渠道，甚⾄能够借⼊ “ ⽆限 ” 规模的资⾦池。这⼀⽬标与当下
⾼企的债券收益率形成鲜明反差：在投资者对偿债能⼒的担忧尚未消退之际，公司能否以更低
成本持续获得融资，仍有待市场检验。
甲⻣⽂、软银等 AI 相关企业 CDS ⾛⾼，信⽤⻛险向⾏业扩散
CoreWeave 并⾮唯⼀遭遇信⽤市场压⼒的 AI 相关企业。

### Page 3

周四盘中，甲⻣⽂五年期 CDS 利差报 288 个基点，与前⼀交易⽇持平，但较⼀周前的 260 个基点
扩⼤ 28 个基点；软银五年期 CDS 利差单⽇扩⼤ 24 个基点⾄ 424 个基点。与此同时，英伟达、意
法半导体和 SpaceX 的 CDS 利差也明显⾛⾼。
据 LSEG 及 S&P Global Market Intelligence 数据，亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 和 Meta 的 CDS 利差也升⾄
历史⾼位，不过整体上升速度慢于甲⻣⽂和 CoreWeave 。这些科技巨头的信⽤保险成本同样受
到 AI 投资融资⻛险重估的影响。
CDS 利差衡量投资者为防范债务违约⻛险所需⽀付的成本。利差⾛阔意味着市场要求更⾼的⻛
险补偿，但不等于企业即将违约，也不能将不同公司的 CDS 利差直接视为可相互⽐较的违约概
率。
多家 AI 相关企业的信⽤保险成本上升，反映出投资者正在重新审视 AI 基础设施投资的融资⽅式。
甲⻣⽂等企业的⼤规模借款需求受到关注， CoreWeave 则因负债沉重、仍未实现盈利且⾼度依

赖外部融资，成为市场压⼒最集中的案例。
路透社指出，美国通胀数据⾼于预期、市场对未来利率⾛向的担忧升温，加剧了投资者对 AI 巨额
资本开⽀回报周期的疑虑。随着⾏业越来越多地依赖债务⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流为 AI 建设融资，市场关
注点正从算⼒需求增⻓本⾝，转向相关企业能否及时创造⾜够利润和现⾦流，以覆盖不断增加
的利息⽀出。
约 500 亿美元债务压顶， CoreWeave 的扩张模式遭拷问
CoreWeave ⾯临的核⼼问题，是资本开⽀增⻓与现⾦流、融资能⼒之间的平衡。
媒体指出，这家 AI 云计算公司背负近 500 亿美元债务，且仍处于亏损状态。公司依靠⼤规模采购
英伟达 GPU 、建设数据中⼼并扩充算⼒容量来满⾜客⼾需求，⽽这类投资通常需要在收⼊逐步
兑现之前投⼊⼤量资⾦。
CoreWeave 已⼤举增加资本开⽀，以抢占快速增⻓的 AI 算⼒市场。在需求强劲的阶段，举债扩
张能够帮助企业提前建设基础设施，并利⽤⻓期客⼾合同⽀撑投资计划。但数据中⼼建设周期
⻓、设备投⼊⼤，⼀旦融资成本上升、项⽬延期或客⼾需求增⻓不及预期，企业就可能⾯临资

⾦缺⼝和偿债压⼒。
CoreWeave 的问题因此不只是债务规模⾼，⽽是市场正在质疑其未来现⾦流能否覆盖资本开
⽀、利息⽀出及其他经营成本。债券收益率上升⼜会进⼀步抬⾼新增融资的⻔槛，使这⼀问题
更加突出。
CoreWeave 的管理层则强调订单储备充⾜，并认为市场对其财务状况存在误判。但订单储备并
不等同于已经收到的现⾦：⻓期合同能够提供⼀定的收⼊可⻅性，却不能⾃动消除建设成本超
⽀、交付延迟、融资条件变化及客⼾集中度等⻛险。投资者需要判断的是，这些合同最终能否
转化为⾜够的现⾦流和可持续利润。
英伟达为何出⾯ “ 灭⽕ ” ，供应商融资与客⼾需求交织
⻩仁勋公开为 CoreWeave 释放信⼼，背后是英伟达与这家 AI 云服务商之间紧密的商业和资本联
系。
英伟达持有 CoreWeave 约 10% 的股份，是其第⼆⼤股东。同时， CoreWeave 也是英伟达 GPU 的
重要采购⽅，其商业模式依赖采购英伟达芯⽚、建设数据中⼼，再将算⼒租赁给 AI 开发商和⼤型
科技企业。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
如果 CoreWeave 融资受阻，后续数据中⼼建设、 GPU 采购及算⼒交付都可能受到影响。即便现
有订单不会⽴即取消，融资成本上升也可能改变项⽬建设节奏和资本回报预期，进⽽影响市场
对英伟达 AI 需求增⻓的判断。
更敏感的问题在于，供应商投资客⼾、客⼾采购供应商产品的安排，容易引发市场对 “ 循环融资 ”
的担忧。
这类交易本⾝并不意味着存在不当⾏为，但当企业依赖借款购买供应商的产品，⽽供应商⼜持
有企业股权时，投资者会追问：产业链的增⻓究竟有多少来⾃最终客⼾的真实需求，⼜有多少
依赖融资推动的持续扩张？
⻩仁勋强调 CoreWeave 的融资成本正在下降，并明确表⽰⽆需英伟达兜底，既是在维护重要合
作伙伴的市场信⼼，也是在避免市场将双⽅的商业联系直接解读为英伟达必须承担 CoreWeave

债务⻛险。
不过，这番表态并不能替代对 CoreWeave 资产负债表的审视。英伟达持有股权并不等于对其债
务提供担保，⻩仁勋的乐观判断也⽆法直接解决公司的融资需求、利息负担和现⾦流问题。
AI 投资回报能否覆盖巨额资本开⽀，成为市场核⼼问题
CoreWeave 的信⽤⻛波正在成为 AI 基础设施投资的⼀次压⼒测试。
在⽣成式 AI 需求快速增⻓的背景下，科技企业和算⼒运营商⼤举建设数据中⼼，试图抢占未来市
场。但基础设施投⼊往往先于收⼊兑现，尤其是依赖外部融资的新型云服务商，需要不断借款
购买 GPU 、建设机房并⽀付能源和运营成本。
当利率预期上升、债券投资者要求更⾼⻛险补偿时，这套模式就会⾯临更严格的检验。融资成
本增加不仅会压缩利润，还可能迫使企业放慢扩建速度；如果扩建放缓，⼜可能影响未来收⼊
增⻓和既有投资的回报。
德意志银⾏策略师  Jim Reid 指出，这并⾮⾸次因 AI 的担忧⽽经历显著的市场动荡，但随着 AI 资
本开⽀周期越来越多地由债务、⽽⾮⾃由现⾦流⽀撑，投资者必须更加重视信⽤市场的价格信

号。 “ 整体⽽⾔， AI 相关的融资成本尚不算⾼，但在⼀些边缘领域已出现令⼈担忧的迹象。 ”
这⼀判断凸显了⾏业⾯临的关键⽭盾：算⼒需求增⻓，并不⾃动等于算⼒运营商能够获得⾜够
利润；订单增⻓，也不必然意味着债务能够得到轻松偿还。
对 CoreWeave ⽽⾔，真正的考验是能否控制债务增⻓、持续获得融资，并将庞⼤的订单储备转
化为可持续的经营现⾦流。对英伟达及整个 AI 产业链⽽⾔，市场关注点则正在从 “ 还需要多少算
⼒ ” ，转向 “ 谁来为算⼒建设买单，以及这些投资最终能否产⽣⾜够回报 ” 。
⻩仁勋试图安抚市场，但 CoreWeave 债券收益率⾼企、信⽤保险费率创纪录，以及甲⻣⽂、软
银等企业 CDS 利差⾛⾼，说明投资者对 AI 融资模式的审视正在深⼊。未来， AI 产业链的估值不
仅取决于算⼒需求能否持续增⻓，也将越来越取决于企业能否以可承受的融资成本，将这场资
本开⽀竞赛转化为真实、可持续的现⾦流。

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-12

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026

for a changing

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

Document 249: IEA加速释放1亿桶原油储备，并承诺优先释放“2000万桶柴油”

Source PDF: all report pdf/IEA加速释放1亿桶原油储备，并承诺优先释放“2000万桶柴油”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

IEA 成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺优先 “ 尽快 ” 释放
其中 2000 万桶柴油。但这批储量本质上是对既有承诺的加速兑现，摩根⼤通和欧盟官员均
明确表⽰并⾮新增⼲预。与此同时，霍尔⽊兹海峡局势持续紧张， WTI 本周累计上涨约
6% ，储备释放对油价的压制效果⼗分有限。
摘要：
国际能源署（ IEA ）成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺 " 尽
快 " 释放其中 2000 万桶柴油。然⽽，这批储备本质上是对既有承诺的加速兑现，⽽⾮新⼀轮⼲预
⾏动。
据彭博报道， IEA 理事会于当地时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇周四召开会议，会后发表声明称，成员国将加快分
配剩余储量，包括 " 尽快 " 释放 2000 万桶柴油。 IEA 同时指出，由于部分成员国释放量超出初始承
诺，最终实际释放总量将超过 3 ⽉宣布的 4 亿桶上限。
油价对此反应有限。 WTI 原油本周累计上涨约 6% ，周四单⽇涨幅达 3% ，⽬前交投于每桶 98 美元

下⽅；布伦特原油则稳定在每桶 106 美元附近。霍尔⽊兹海峡局势持续紧张，仍是市场主要上⾏
⻛险来源。
1 亿桶是旧账
IEA 于 2026 年 3 ⽉宣布释放 4 亿桶⽯油储备，以应对伊朗战争引发的供应中断。截⾄⽬前，成员
国已释放 3.3 亿桶，此次 1 亿桶系补⾜剩余缺⼝。
此前，摩根⼤通分析师明确指出，这批储备释放 " 在很⼤程度上是加速兑现此前已有承诺，⽽⾮
代表规模达 1 亿桶的全新⼲预⾏动 " 。
欧盟委员会发⾔⼈亦表态称，计划中的储备释放 " 必须在 3 ⽉批准的总量范围内推进 " 。德国经济
部确认将参与释放，但未披露任何新增规模。
美国施压，欧洲划定边界
此次会议背后，美欧之间存在明显分歧。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
据彭博报道，美国曾⾮正式游说追加释放，以压制国内飙升的柴油价格，但在本次会议上并未
提出正式提案。知情⼈⼠透露，欧洲官员对于此时追加释放持怀疑态度。
美国能源部在声明中表⽰， " 以柴油为重点的进⼀步⾏动正在审查和推进中 " 。
特朗普政府措辞更为强硬。能源部声明称： " 特朗普政府持续要求欧洲国家履⾏此前承诺，以减
少全球能源市场供应中断、帮助压低能源价格。继特朗普总统在 G7 上发出呼吁后，所有⾝为 IEA
成员的欧洲国家均已承诺⽴即完成 3 ⽉承诺并释放额外桶量。 "
IEA ⽅⾯拒绝就声明作进⼀步说明。
在柴油问题上，欧盟成员国内部讨论显⽰，⽅向可⾏但规模预计有限。截⾄⽬前，尚⽆欧盟成
员国明确承诺在 G7 设定的 20 天窗⼝内额外释放柴油库存。波兰能源部⻓⽶沃什 · 莫蒂卡表⽰，若

各⽅达成共识，波兰已准备好释放⽯油和柴油库存。法国和意⼤利可能考虑在 3 ⽉承诺基础上进
⼀步释放部分柴油，但尚未作出最终决定。
柴油危机是核⼼压⼒点
IEA 此次将柴油单独列出，折射出当前市场最紧迫的⽭盾所在。
伊朗战争与乌克兰冲突导致炼油设施受损、油轮航运受阻，柴油价格在多个市场创下历史新
⾼。据彭博报道，特朗普曾警告，若 G7 国家不向市场投放更多柴油，他可能禁⽌美国柴油出
⼝，并已宣布与莫斯科达成供应协议。
IEA 声明显⽰，成员国⽬前持有的公共应急⽯油库存约为 11 亿桶，其中柴油超过 2 亿桶。
美国能源信息署（ EIA ）数据显⽰，上周美国原油库存下降 860 万桶，为今年 2 ⽉以来最⼤单周降
幅，馏分油库存亦减少 250 万桶，降⾄季节性新低。
霍尔⽊兹⻛险持续升温
储备释放的市场效果，正被地缘局势的不确定性所抵消。
据彭博报道，美国中央司令部周四确认，本周在霍尔⽊兹海峡沉没的⼀艘集装箱船系遭伊朗⾰
命卫队投射物击中。这艘悬挂新加坡旗帜的 GFS Pride 在被拖往岸边途中沉没。

尽管如此，过去 24 ⼩时内仍有逾 40 艘船只通过霍尔⽊兹海峡，显⽰航运并未完全中断。
欧亚集团分析师在研报中写道： " 冲突很可能进⼀步升级。 " 该机构将局势激化的概率从 35% 上调
⾄ 65% ，并指出 " 当前的对峙僵局对美国⽽⾔已不可持续 " 。
限免

► 博通3Q26營收達 295.9億美元 ，年增86%，
優 於 市 場 預 期。AI 半 導 體 營 收 達167 億美
元，年增221%，占整體營收比重逾五成。然
而，短期股價表現卻趨向震盪 ，主因為4Q26
營收與營業利益率指引未大幅超越已偏高的
市場預期。
► 觀察公司目前的Google TPU、Meta MTIA、
OpenAI與Anthropic 專案仍持續推進 ，博通
訂單需求未見轉弱 。公司營收成長主要來自
客製化XPU與AI networking，管理層預期未
來數年AI網路產品以及光通訊相關產品的營
收成長速度可與 XPU相當，顯示AI營收成長
並非完全依賴單一 ASIC專案，將有望支持營
收能見度。
► 整體而言，本次財報訊號偏正向，從未來2年
的AI營收展望來看 ，除大型CSP與模型業者
對 客 製 化AI 晶 片 的 投 資 未 見 放 緩， 在 AI
networking產品的營收成長加速下 ，料將進
一步提升博通在AI叢集中的內含價值。

博通財報接力AI浪潮，客製化晶片與AI網路支撐營收能見度擴張

### Page 6

6
投資焦點金融股
► 隨著AI資本支出繼續創高 ，整體AI產業的自
由現金流卻面臨更大壓力 。自2022年底AI資
本支出週期啟動以來 ，獲利與現金流的分歧
便日趨明顯 ，截至2Q26，4大CSP過去12個
月的淨利為5,990億美元，而自由現金流僅剩
1,690億美元。
► AI資料中心持續建設 ，最新統計包括亞馬遜
等AI資料中心營運商 相關企業 發債量2026年
至今已較去年 增加約98%。代表在企業自由
現金流持續放緩的背景下 ，AI企業的對外融
資需求已快速擴增 ，並同步挹注了金融業的
手續費與交易收入。
► 隨著投銀業務 、股債交易及財富 管理業務表
現強勁 ，2Q26金融 股財報普遍優於 市場 預
期，故市場紛紛上修對於金融股的獲利成長
預估。此外，在年底前聯準會仍具升息1碼空
間，加上殖利率環境維持高檔的環境下 ，亦
有利金融股延續基本面成長趨勢 ，並有望持
續獲得市場資金青睞。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 金融股獲利成長持續上修4大CSP淨利與自由現金流呈現分岐，發債融資擴增

### Page 7

投資焦點工業股
► 2026年第2季財報數據顯示 ，4大CSP之AI相
關資本支出持續增加，並同步提高未來2年的
財務指引 。目前市場普遍預期 ，今年底AI相
關資本支出將達到約 9,070億美元 ，2027年
底更有望超過1.2兆美元。反映AI算力需求迅
速攀升 ，相關基礎設施需求遠超供給 。過去
數季，大型CSP企業幾乎每季皆調高資本支
出預期 ，推動產業持續進行 「AI軍備競賽 」
以確保未來競爭優勢。
► 觀察最新8月ISM製造業指數雖較上個月略為
下滑，但新訂單 、新出口訂單僅小幅回落 ，
整體指數仍維持在 50以上的擴張區間 ，顯示
製造業動能仍保持強勁 。加上耐久財訂單年
增率亦維持高檔 ，代表AI資本支出擴增趨勢
帶來的外溢效應未有改變。
► 因此，市場普遍預估 2026年工業股獲利成長
將持續攀升 ，除了反映資本支出擴大外 ，地
緣政治反覆亦將加大 國防工業需求 ，令整體
工業類股後續有望獲得更多資金進駐。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 工業獲利成長強勁攀升AI資本支出快速擴增，推升製造業相關數據動能

8
股市
 全球主要國家債務攀升 ，利率創高添股市評價修正風險 ，加上科技股舉債投入資本支出 ，未來獲利
與變現能力備受關注 。美伊衝突加劇不確定性 ，短線可透過布局醫療與公用事業等防禦 股，降低市
場震盪風險 ，科技及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利等基本面支持長線發展 ，採逢回分批布局 ，並
分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，日本半導體股
分批布局較穩健，歐洲國防軍工仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融、工業等產業機會
區域：日本銀行股、日本半導體股、歐洲
金融、國防
債市
 美國30年期公債殖利率維持相對高檔 ，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國10年期公債利率
將於4.5%-5.0%震盪，可逢利率走升時 ，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質 較佳，具穩
定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者 ，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業 等產業。

 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會9月升息預期回升，美元短線高檔震盪。
 日本銀行釋出鷹派訊號，9月升息可能性升高，有利日圓止貶回升。
日圓震盪偏升
商品
 中東衝突反覆 ，荷姆茲海峽短期恢復正常通行機率偏低 ，加上石油庫存探低 ，預期今年能源仍呈供
不應求態勢，油價維持偏高水位。
 美國財務壓力與去美元化之兩個長期結構性問題依然存在，央行買盤提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金區間操作，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 殖利率走升加劇市場震盪，短期防禦待拉回布局機會

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Document 273: Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Next Generation EU_ The programme is coming to an end, but its effects on economic activity will endure (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

23 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
NEXT GENERATION EU: THE PROGRAMME IS COMING TO AN END, BUT ITS EFFECTS ON ECONOMIC ACTIVITY WILL
ENDURE
Guillaume Derrien
A pillar of the NextGenerationEU programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) will expire on December 31, 2026. As of the
end of August 2026, slightly more than three-quarters of the total budget had been disbursed (EUR 440 billion out of EUR 573 billion).
Disbursing the remaining quarter (EUR 133 billion) by the end of the year will require a significant increase compared with the histo-

rical pace (EUR 90 billion per year). The goal of channeling these funds toward productive investment appears to have been achieved.
Tangible results for investment in Europe
According to Eurostat, public spending co-financed by the RRF totaled EUR 310 billion between 2020 and 2025. Nearly three-quarters (74%)
consisted of capital expenditures, including EUR 106 billion in direct public investment and EUR 122 billion in capital transfers to the private

sector (such as grants). This spending supported investment by businesses and households.
The list of major programmes funded by the RFF, published by the European Commission 1, shows that, within this scope, nearly two-thirds of
the funding went to transportation (primarily rail), energy infrastructure, and digital infrastructure (see Chart 1).
Another notable point: capital expenditures co-financed by the RRF increased significantly, reaching 0.4% of GDP for the European Union as a

whole in 2025 (see Chart 2). There are significant disparities among Member States, primarily due to differences in the amounts allocated to
them. Croatia (1.9%), Greece (1.2%), Italy (1%), and Spain (0.7%) received significant support, in contrast to France and Germany (0.1%). These
figures are consistent with the IMF’s estimate that the RRF added approximately 0.3 percentage points (pp) to EU growth in 2025 (more than
1 pp in Greece and Croatia, around 0.8 pp in Italy, and 0.5 pp in Spain) 2. Furthermore, as the first major joint European borrowing initiative, the

RRF made it possible to finance investment without increasing national budget deficits, giving several countries (Spain, Italy, Greece, Portugal)
valuable breathing room to consolidate their public finances.
1 Recovery and Resilience Scoreboard
2 The EU’s Recovery and Resilience Facility: Experience and Lessons for the Next Multiannual Financial Framework, WP/26/114, June 2026
SOURCE: EUROPEAN COMMISSION, Recovery and Resilience Scorecard

What impact on EU’s economic activity can we expect once this programme ends?
At first glance, effects will be limited. The end of the programme is expected to weigh on both public and private investment. However, the
strengthening of economic activity that we anticipate (see details in our latest Ecoperspectives – Q3 2026) rests primarily on German public

investment programmes and structural drivers (investment in AI, defence spending).
The ECB makes the same point in its September macroeconomic projections 3. It emphasises that the end of the RRF is expected to weigh
on public investment in 2027, while it anticipates an acceleration in business investment. Furthermore, RRF funds are being absorbed on
the ground only gradually. Since most disbursements are concentrated toward the end of the programme, the effects of the RRF will extend

beyond 2026.

Document 296: Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Supply chain risks are changing. Shouldn’t financing change too_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

As climate risks have a larger impact on supply chains, the
need to ﬁnance resilience-building initiatives is increasing.
Banks have an important role to play by expanding their
ﬁnancing toolkit, while companies with proactive resilience
strategies can gain greater access to capital
Coco Zhang
As climate risks become supply chain risks, ﬁnancing supply chain resilience is
oﬀering opportunities for banks and companies to explore together
Climate and weather-related shocks are a growing source of supply

chain risk. In August, the Rhine River’s water levels hit a record low
since 1880, hindering trade ﬂows across Europe. In 2024, Hurricane
Helene aﬀected global tech and medical supply chains by ﬂooding
key ultra-pure quartz production facilities in the US. The upcoming
“very strong” El Niño could substantially disrupt temperature and
precipitation patterns across the globe next year, putting, for
example, food production and trade at risk.
These events underscore the need to build supply chain resilience.

Extreme weather is now a more impactful part of the supply chain

### Page 2

risk landscape alongside geopolitics and other risks.
Yet signiﬁcant gaps remain. First, while the awareness of supply
chain climate risks is rising, many companies have yet to
acknowledge their ﬁnancial materiality. Second, the uptake of
ﬁnancing solutions has not kept pace with supply chain resilience
needs, despite trade ﬁnance supporting around 90% of global
trade. This leaves ﬁnancial institutions well positioned to expand
ﬁnancing solutions that support corporate supply chain resilience.

Below is how they can do it.
Expanding banks’ toolkit for supply chain
resilience
1. Broaden the use of sustainable ﬁnance
Today, the most common way banks support supply chain
resilience is through sustainable ﬁnance – in other words,
sustainability-labelled debt. However, scaling up this market
remains challenging because supply chain resilience is not a
standalone category under widely used frameworks such as the
International Capital Market Association’s (ICMA’s) Green Bond

Principles and Green Loan Principles. This makes it harder for issuers
to ﬁnance projects under a dedicated supply chain resilience
theme. As a result, there is also limited data on the total volume of
sustainable ﬁnance supporting supply chain resilience. The global
green bond use-of-proceeds allocation data, for example,
demonstrates this challenge.
Global green bond use-of-proceeds
allocation, January-August 2026

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF
But at the same time, this creates an opportunity. Supply chains
are closely linked to many existing sustainable ﬁnance themes.
Projects that strengthen supply chain resilience can often be
ﬁnanced under established categories such as climate adaptation
(e.g. resilient infrastructure and information systems), sustainable
water management (e.g. ﬂood mitigation), circular economy, and
so on. This requires banks to better understand which supply chain

challenges matter most to each client and identify the
sustainability solutions best suited to their needs.
2. Look beyond labelled sustainable debt
The opportunity to ﬁnance supply chain resilience extends well
beyond traditional sustainable debt markets. Here are some
examples:
Supply chain resilience ﬁnance
The traditional supply chain and trade ﬁnance solutions
can be tailored to integrate resilience objectives. For
example, guarantees can support cross-border trade in

climate-resilient goods, while preferential ﬁnancing terms
can reward companies that actively manage supply chain
risks. By incorporating supply chain risk assessments into
ﬁnancing programmes and linking working capital
solutions to supplier resilience, these products can help
companies better withstand disruptions and support long-
term growth.
Climate and resilience tech ﬁnance
